Palmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_W._Palmer), all of whom had ties to Clinton when he was Governor of Arkansas.[^cite_note-213] Former Clinton [HUD Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HUD_Secretary) [Henry Cisneros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cisneros), who pleaded guilty to lying to the FBI, was also among Clinton's pardons.[^cite_note-214]

#### Campaign finance controversies

Further information: [Lincoln Bedroom for contributors controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Bedroom_for_contributors_controversy) and [1996 United States campaign finance controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_United_States_campaign_finance_controversy)

In February 1997 it was discovered upon documents being released by the [Clinton Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_Administration) that 938 people had stayed at the White House and that 821 of them had made donations to the [Democratic Party](5043544.md) and got the opportunity to stay in the Lincoln bedroom as a result of the donations.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-washingtonpost.com-216] Some donors included [Steven Spielberg](26940.md), [Tom Hanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Hanks), [Jane Fonda](63520.md), and [Judy Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Collins). Top donors also got golf games and morning jogs with Clinton as a result of the contributions.[^cite_note-washingtonpost.com-216] [Janet Reno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Reno) was called on to investigate the matter by [Trent Lott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trent_Lott), but she refused.[^cite_note-217]

In 1996, it was found that several Chinese foreigners made contributions to Clinton's reelection campaign and the [Democratic National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_National_Committee) with the backing of the [People's Republic of China](5405.md). Some of them also attempted to donate to Clinton's defense fund.[^cite_note-218] This violated United States law forbidding non-American citizens from making campaign contributions. Clinton and [Al Gore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Gore) also allegedly met with the foreign donors.[^cite_note-223] A Republican investigation led by [Fred Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fred_Thompson) found that Clinton was targeted by the Chinese government. However, Democratic senators [Joe Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Lieberman) and [John Glenn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Glenn) said that the evidence showed that China only targeted congressional elections and not presidential elections.[^cite_note-224]

### Military and foreign affairs

Main article: [Foreign policy of the Clinton administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_Clinton_administration)

#### Somalia



[Col.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonel_\(United_States\)) [Paul Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_J._Fletcher), [USAF](32090.md) and Clinton speak before boarding [Air Force One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_One), November 4, 1999.

American troops had first entered [Somalia](27358.md) during the [George H. W. Bush administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_George_H._W._Bush_administration) in response to a humanitarian crisis and [civil war](3070332.md). Though initially involved to assist humanitarian efforts, the Clinton administration shifted the objectives set out in the mission and began pursuing a policy of attempting to neutralize Somali warlords. In 1993, during the [Battle of Mogadishu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Mogadishu_\(1993\)), [two U.S. helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MH-60_Black_Hawk) were shot down by [rocket-propelled grenade](26286.md) attacks to their [tail rotors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tail_rotor), trapping soldiers behind enemy lines. This resulted in an urban battle that killed 18 American soldiers, wounded 73 others, and resulted in one being taken prisoner.[^cite_note-NPR-225] Television news programs depicted the supporters of warlord [Mohammed Aidid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Aidid) desecrating the corpses of troops.[^cite_note-NPR-225] The backlash resulting from the incident prompted a drop in support for American intervention in the country and coincided with a more cautious use of troops throughout the rest of the Clinton administration.[^cite_note-NPR-225] Following a subsequent national security policy review, U.S. forces were withdrawn from Somalia and later conflicts were approached with fewer soldiers on the ground.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227]

#### Rwanda

In April 1994, [genocide](676659.md) broke out in [Rwanda](25645.md). Intelligence reports indicate that Clinton was aware a "final solution to eliminate all [Tutsis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tutsi)" was underway, long before the administration publicly used the word "genocide".[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] Fearing a reprisal of the events in Somalia the previous year, Clinton chose not to intervene.[^cite_note-231] Clinton has called his failure to intervene one of his main foreign policy failings, saying "I don't think we could have ended the violence, but I think we could have cut it down. And I regret it."[^cite_note-232]

#### Bosnia and Herzegovina



Clinton with the U.S. delegation to Bosnia and Air Force personnel in a flight to [Tuzla](524638.md) on December 22, 1997. Clinton is seen alongside future 46th president [Joe Biden](145422.md).

In 1993 and 1994, Clinton pressured Western European leaders to adopt a strong military policy against [Bosnian Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_Serbs) during the [Bosnian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_War). This strategy faced staunch opposition from the [United Nations](31769.md), [NATO](21133.md) allies, and congressional Republicans, leading Clinton to adopt a more diplomatic approach.[^cite_note-233] In 1995, U.S. and NATO aircraft [bombed Bosnian Serb targets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Deliberate_Force) to halt attacks on UN safe zones and pressure them into a peace accord that would end the [Bosnian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_war). Clinton deployed U.S. peacekeepers to Bosnia in late 1995, to uphold the subsequent [Dayton Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayton_Agreement).[^cite_note-234]

#### Northern Irish peace talks



Clinton shaking hands with [Gerry Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerry_Adams) outside a business in East Belfast, November 30, 1995

In 1992, before his presidency, Clinton proposed sending a peace envoy to [Northern Ireland](21265.md), but this was dropped to avoid tensions with the British government. In November 1995, in a ceasefire during [the Troubles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Troubles), Clinton became the first president to visit Northern Ireland, examining both of the two divided communities of [Belfast](5046.md).[^cite_note-235] Despite [unionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unionism_in_Ireland) criticism, Clinton used his visit as a way to negotiate an end to the violent conflict, playing a key role in the [peace talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Ireland_peace_process) that produced the [Good Friday Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday_Agreement) in 1998.[^cite_note-236]



Clinton plays the saxophone presented to him by Russian president [Boris Yeltsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Yeltsin) at a private dinner in Russia, January 13, 1994.

#### Iran

Clinton sought to continue the Bush administration's policy of limiting Iranian influence in the Middle East, which he laid out in the [dual containment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_containment) strategy. In 1994, Clinton declared that Iran was a "[state sponsor of terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Sponsors_of_Terrorism)" and a "rogue state", marking the first time that an American president used that term.[^cite_note-237] Subsequent executive orders heavily sanctioned Iran's oil industry and banned almost all trade between U.S. companies and the Iranian government. In February 1996, the Clinton administration agreed to pay Iran US\$131.8 million (equivalent to \$270.56 million in 2025) in settlement to discontinue a case brought by Iran in 1989 against the U.S. in the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) after the shooting down of [Iran Air Flight 655](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Air_Flight_655) by the U.S. Navy [guided missile cruiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guided_missile_cruiser).[^cite_note-238]

#### Iraq

In Clinton's [1998 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_State_of_the_Union_Address), he warned Congress that Iraqi dictator [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) was building an arsenal of chemical, biological and nuclear weapons.[^cite_note-239] However, there was no evidence for that claim.[^cite_note-240]

Clinton signed the [Iraq Liberation Act of 1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Liberation_Act_of_1998) on October 31, 1998, which instituted a policy of "regime change" against Iraq, though it explicitly stated it did not provide for direct intervention on the part of American military forces.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242] The administration then launched a four-day bombing campaign named [Operation Desert Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Desert_Fox), lasting from December 16 to 19, 1998. At the end of this operation Clinton announced that "So long as Saddam remains in power, he will remain a threat to his people, his region, and the world. With our allies, we must pursue a strategy to contain him and to constrain his weapons of mass destruction program, while working toward the day Iraq has a government willing to live at peace with its people and with its neighbors."[^cite_note-243] American and British aircraft in the Iraq no-fly zones attacked hostile Iraqi air defenses 166 times in 1999 and 78 times in 2000.[^cite_note-244]

#### Osama bin Laden

Capturing Osama bin Laden was an objective of the U.S. government during the Clinton presidency (and continued to be until [bin Laden's death in 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Osama_bin_Laden)).[^cite_note-245] Despite claims by [Mansoor Ijaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansoor_Ijaz) and Sudanese officials that the Sudanese government had offered to arrest and extradite bin Laden, and that U.S. authorities rejected each offer,[^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Times-246] the [9/11 Commission Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9/11_Commission_Report) stated that "we have not found any reliable evidence to support the Sudanese claim".[^cite_note-247]

In response to a 1996 State Department warning about bin Laden[^cite_note-248] and the [1998 bombings of U.S. embassies in East Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_United_States_embassy_bombings) by al-Qaeda (which killed 224 people, including 12 Americans), Clinton ordered several military missions to capture or kill bin Laden, all of which were unsuccessful. In August 1998, Clinton [ordered cruise missile strikes on terrorist targets in Afghanistan and Sudan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Infinite_Reach), targeting the [Al-Shifa pharmaceutical factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shifa_pharmaceutical_factory) in Sudan, which was suspected of assisting bin Laden in making chemical weapons, and bin Laden's terrorist training camps in Afghanistan. The factory was destroyed by the attack, resulting in the death of one employee and the wounding of 11 other people.[^cite_note-249] After the destruction of the factory, there was a medicine shortage in Sudan due to the plant providing 50 percent of Sudan's medicine, and the destruction of the plant led to a shortage of chloroquine, a drug which is used to treat malaria.[^cite_note-250] U.S. officials later acknowledged that there was no evidence the plant was acknowledging manufacturing or storing nerve gas.[^cite_note-251] The attack provoked criticism of Clinton from journalists and academics including [Christopher Hitchens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Hitchens),[^cite_note-252] [Seymour Hersh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seymour_Hersh),[^cite_note-253] [Max Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Taylor_\(psychologist\)),[^cite_note-254] and others.[^cite_note-255]

#### Kosovo



Clinton during a briefing on Kosovo, March 31, 1999

In the midst of a brutal crackdown on [ethnic Albanian separatists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Liberation_Army) in the province of [Kosovo](17391.md) by the [Federal Republic of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia), Clinton authorized the use of U.S. Armed Forces in a NATO bombing campaign against Yugoslavia in 1999, named [Operation Allied Force](322473.md).[^cite_note-256] The stated reasoning behind the intervention was to stop the [ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) (and what the Clinton administration labeled [genocide](12441.md))[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] of Albanians by Yugoslav anti-guerilla military units. General [Wesley Clark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Clark) was [Supreme Allied Commander of NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Allied_Commander_Europe) and oversaw the mission. With [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1244](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1244), the bombing campaign ended on June 10, 1999. The resolution placed Kosovo under UN administration and authorized a [peacekeeping force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Force) to be deployed to the region.[^cite_note-259] NATO announced its soldiers all survived combat,[^cite_note-roblect-260] though two died in an [Apache helicopter](37746.md) crash.[^cite_note-261] Journalists in the popular press criticized genocide statements by the Clinton administration as false and greatly exaggerated.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263] Prior to the bombing campaign on March 24, 1999, estimates showed that the number of civilians killed in the over year long [conflict in Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_war) had been approximately 1,800, with critics asserting that little or no evidence existed of genocide.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] In a post-war inquiry, the [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md) noted "the patterns of the expulsions and the vast increase in lootings, killings, rape, kidnappings and pillage once the NATO air war began on March 24."[^cite_note-266] In 2001, the [UN-supervised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Interim_Administration_Mission_in_Kosovo) [Supreme Court of Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Kosovo) ruled that genocide (the [intent to destroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocidal_intent) a people) did not take place, but recognized "a systematic campaign of terror, including murders, rapes, arsons and severe maltreatments" with the intention being the forceful departure of the Albanian population.[^cite_note-267] The term "ethnic cleansing" was used as an alternative to "genocide" to denote not just ethnically motivated murder but also displacement, though critics charge there is little difference.[^cite_note-268] [Slobodan Milošević](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slobodan_Milo%C5%A1evi%C4%87), the president of Yugoslavia at the time of the atrocities, was [eventually brought to trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_of_Slobodan_Milo%C5%A1evi%C4%87) before the [International Criminal Tribunal for the former Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Tribunal_for_the_former_Yugoslavia) in [the Hague](30269.md) on charges including [crimes against humanity](61099.md) and war crimes for his role in the war.[^cite_note-Milosevic_Charges-269] He died in 2006, before the completion of the trial.[^cite_note-Milosevic_Charges-269][^cite_note-270]

#### China

See also: [1996 United States campaign finance controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_United_States_campaign_finance_controversy)

Clinton aimed to increase trade with China, minimizing import tariffs and offering the country [most favoured nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Most_favoured_nation) status in 1993, his administration minimized tariff levels in Chinese imports. Clinton initially conditioned extension of this status on [human rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China) reforms, but ultimately decided to extend the status despite a lack of reform in the specified areas, including free emigration, treatment of prisoners in terms of international human rights, and observation of human rights specified by UN resolutions, among others.[^cite_note-271]

Relations were damaged briefly by the [American bombing of the Chinese embassy in Belgrade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_bombing_of_the_Chinese_embassy_in_Belgrade) in May 1999. Clinton apologized for the bombing, stating it was accidental.[^cite_note-272]

Israeli prime minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak), President Clinton and Palestinian leader [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) at [Camp David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David), July 2000

On October 10, 2000, Clinton signed into law the [United States–China Relations Act of 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States%E2%80%93China_Relations_Act_of_2000), which granted [permanent normal trade relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_normal_trade_relations) (PNTR) trade status to China.[^cite_note-273] The president asserted that free trade would gradually open China to democratic reform.[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275]

In encouraging Congress to approve the agreement and China's accession to the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) (WTO), Clinton stated that more trade with China would advance America's economic interests, saying that "economically, this agreement is the equivalent of a one-way street. It requires China to open its markets—with a fifth of the world's population, potentially the biggest markets in the world—to both our products and services in unprecedented new ways."[^cite_note-276]

#### Israeli-Palestinian conflict

Summit of the Peacemakers in Sharm el-Sheikh, March 1996

World Leaders attending the Sharm El Sheikh Summit for Peacemakers. From left: [Helmut Kohl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helmut_Kohl), [King Husein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_of_Jordan), [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres), Clinton, [Hosni Mubarak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosni_Mubarak), [Boris Yeltsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Yeltsin) and [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) in [Sharm El Sheikh](419335.md), March 1996

Clinton attempted to end the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md). Secret negotiations mediated by Clinton between Israeli Prime Minister [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) and [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) (PLO) Chairman [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) led to a historic declaration of peace in September 1993, called the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), which were signed at the White House on September 13. The agreement led to the [Israel–Jordan peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty) in 1994 and the [Wye River Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wye_River_Memorandum) in October 1998, however, this did not end the conflict. He brought Israeli prime minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) and Palestinian Authority chairman Yasser Arafat together at [Camp David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David) for the [2000 Camp David Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Camp_David_Summit), which lasted 14 days in July.[^cite_note-The_Survivor-58] Following another attempt in December 2000 at [Bolling Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolling_Air_Force_Base), in which the president offered the [Clinton Parameters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Clinton_Parameters), the situation broke down completely after the end of the [Taba Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taba_Summit) and with the start of the [Second Intifada](71717.md).[^cite_note-The_Survivor-58]

### Judicial appointments

Main articles: [Bill Clinton Supreme Court candidates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton_Supreme_Court_candidates) and [List of federal judges appointed by Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_federal_judges_appointed_by_Bill_Clinton)



[Ruth Bader Ginsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Bader_Ginsburg) accepting her nomination to the Supreme Court from President Clinton, 1993

Clinton appointed two justices to the [Supreme Court](31737.md): [Ruth Bader Ginsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Bader_Ginsburg) in 1993[^cite_note-277] and [Stephen Breyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Breyer) in 1994.[^cite_note-278] Both justices went on to serve until the 2020s, leaving a lasting judicial legacy for President Clinton.[^cite_note-279]

Clinton was the first president in history to appoint more women and minority judges than white male judges to the federal courts.[^cite_note-Scherer,_p._85-280] In his eight years in office, 11.6% of Clinton's court of appeals nominees and 17.4% of his district court nominees were black; 32.8% of his court of appeals nominees and 28.5% of his district court nominees were women.[^cite_note-Scherer,_p._85-280]

## Public opinion



Clinton's [approval ratings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_approval_rating) throughout his presidential career (Roper Center)

Throughout Clinton's first term, his job approval rating fluctuated in the 40s and 50s. In his second term, his rating consistently ranged from the high-50s to the high-60s.[^cite_note-281] After his impeachment proceedings in 1998 and 1999, Clinton's rating reached its highest point.[^cite_note-282] According to a [CBS News](359551.md)/*New York Times* poll, Clinton left office with an approval rating of 68 percent, which matched those of Ronald Reagan and Franklin D. Roosevelt as the highest ratings for departing presidents in the modern era.[^cite_note-283] Clinton's average [Gallup poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_Organization) approval rating for his last quarter in office was 61 percent, the highest final quarter rating any president has received for fifty years.[^cite_note-Jan2001Gallup-284] Forty-seven percent of the respondents identified themselves as being Clinton supporters.[^cite_note-Jan2001Gallup-284]

As he was leaving office, a CNN/*[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)*/Gallup poll revealed that 45 percent of Americans said they would miss him; 55 percent thought he "would have something worthwhile to contribute and should remain active in public life"; 68 percent thought he would be remembered more for his "involvement in personal scandal" than for "his accomplishments"; and 58 percent answered "No" to the question "Do you generally think Bill Clinton is honest and trustworthy?"[^cite_note-Jan2001Gallup-284] The same percentage said he would be remembered as either "outstanding" or "above average" as a president, while 22 percent said he would be remembered as "below average" or "poor".[^cite_note-Jan2001Gallup-284] [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) characterized public consensus on Clinton as, "You can't trust him, he's got weak morals and ethics—and he's done a heck of a good job."[^cite_note-langer-285] During his first term, roughly 7 in 10 Americans believed that the media unfairly covered Clinton's character flaws, according to polling.[^cite_note-286]

A year after he left office, a Gallup poll found that 51 percent of respondents said they approved of the overall job Clinton did as president.[^cite_note-287] In May 2006, a CNN poll comparing Clinton's job performance with that of his successor, George W. Bush, found that a strong majority of respondents said Clinton outperformed Bush in six different areas questioned.[^cite_note-288] A June 2006 poll by Gallup found that 61 percent of Americans said they approved of the job Clinton did as president, a 10-point increase from the 2002 poll.[^cite_note-289] Gallup polls in 2007 and 2011 showed that Clinton was regarded by 13 percent of Americans as the greatest president in U.S. history.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291]

In 2010, 69 percent of respondents in a Gallup survey said they approved of the job Clinton did as president, including 47 percent of Republicans and 68 percent of independents. His sudden spike in popularity during this time was attributed to Americans comparing him to then-incumbent Democratic president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), who had low approval ratings.[^cite_note-292] In 2014, 18 percent of respondents in a [Quinnipiac University Polling Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_University_Polling_Institute) poll of American voters regarded Clinton as the best president since World War II, making him the third most popular among postwar presidents, behind John F. Kennedy and Ronald Reagan.[^cite_note-Q_Poll-293] The same poll showed that just 3 percent of American voters regarded Clinton as the worst president since World War II.[^cite_note-Q_Poll-293]

A 2015 poll by *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* asked 162 scholars of the [American Political Science Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Political_Science_Association) to rank all the U.S. presidents in order of greatness. According to their findings, Clinton ranked eighth overall, with a rating of 70 percent.[^cite_note-294]

## Public image

Main article: [Public image of Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_image_of_Bill_Clinton)



Clinton addressing the [British Parliament](13964.md) on November 29, 1995

Clinton was the first [baby boomer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baby_boomers) president.[^cite_note-295] Authors Martin Walker and [Bob Woodward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Woodward) stated that Clinton's innovative use of [sound bite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_bite)-ready dialogue, personal charisma, and public perception-oriented campaigning were a major factor in his high public approval ratings.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] When Clinton played the saxophone on *[The Arsenio Hall Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Arsenio_Hall_Show)*, he was described by some religious conservatives as "the [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV) president".[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] Opponents sometimes referred to him as "Slick Willie", a [nickname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_nicknames_of_presidents_of_the_United_States) which was first applied to him in 1980 by *[Pine Bluff Commercial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pine_Bluff_Commercial)* journalist [Paul Greenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Greenberg_\(journalist\));[^cite_note-mafadgreenberg-300] Greenberg believed that Clinton was abandoning the progressive policies of previous Arkansas governors such as [Winthrop Rockefeller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winthrop_Rockefeller), [Dale Bumpers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dale_Bumpers) and [David Pryor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Pryor).[^cite_note-mafadgreenberg-300] The claim "Slick Willie" would last throughout his presidency.[^cite_note-WaPoNickname-301] His folksy manner led him to be nicknamed [Bubba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bubba) starting from the 1992 presidential election.[^cite_note-\:02-302] Since 2000, he has frequently been referred to as "The Big Dog" or "Big Dog".[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] His prominent role in campaigning for Obama during the [2012 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_presidential_election) and [his widely publicized speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton's_speech_at_the_2012_Democratic_National_Convention) at the [2012 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Democratic_National_Convention), where he officially nominated Obama and criticized Republican nominee [Mitt Romney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitt_Romney) and Republican policies in detail, earned him the nickname "Explainer-in-Chief".[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

Clinton drew strong support from the African American community and insisted that the improvement of race relations would be a major theme of his presidency.[^cite_note-307] In 1998, [Nobel](21201.md) laureate [Toni Morrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toni_Morrison) called Clinton "the first black president", saying, "Clinton displays almost every trope of blackness: single-parent household, born poor, working-class, saxophone-playing, [McDonald's](2480627.md)-and-junk-food-loving boy from Arkansas".[^cite_note-MorrisonNewYorker-308] Morrison noted that Clinton's sex life was scrutinized more than his career accomplishments, and she compared this to the stereotyping and [double standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_standard) that, she said, black people typically endure.[^cite_note-MorrisonNewYorker-308] Many viewed this comparison as unfair and disparaging both to Clinton and to the African-American community.[^cite_note-309]

### Sexual assault and misconduct allegations

Main article: [Bill Clinton sexual assault and misconduct allegations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton_sexual_assault_and_misconduct_allegations)



Clinton and [Monica Lewinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_Lewinsky) on February 28, 1997

Several women have publicly accused Clinton of sexual misconduct, including rape, harassment, and sexual assault. Additionally, some commentators have characterized Clinton's sexual relationship with former White House intern [Monica Lewinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_Lewinsky) as predatory or non-consensual, despite the fact that Lewinsky called the relationship consensual at the time. These allegations have been revisited and lent more credence in 2018, in light of the [#MeToo movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me_Too_movement), with many commentators and Democratic leaders now saying Clinton should have been compelled to resign after the Lewinsky affair.[^cite_note-hallmark1-310][^cite_note-mccoy1-311][^cite_note-312]

In 1994, [Paula Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paula_Jones) initiated a [sexual harassment lawsuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_v._Jones) against Clinton, claiming he had made unwanted advances towards her in 1991; Clinton denied the allegations. In April 1998, the case was initially dismissed by Judge [Susan Webber Wright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Webber_Wright) on the grounds that it lacked legal merit.[^cite_note-JO#\$-313] Jones appealed Webber Wright's ruling, and her suit gained traction following Clinton's admission to having an affair with Monica Lewinsky in August 1998.[^cite_note-314] In 1998, lawyers for Paula Jones released court documents that alleged a pattern of sexual harassment by Clinton when he was Governor of Arkansas. [Robert S. Bennett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_S._Bennett), Clinton's main lawyer for the case, called the filing "a pack of lies" and "an organized campaign to smear the President of the United States" funded by Clinton's political enemies.[^cite_note-315] In October 1998, Clinton's attorneys tentatively offered \$700,000 to settle the case, which was then the \$800,000 which Jones' lawyers sought.[^cite_note-316] Clinton later agreed to an out-of-court settlement and paid Jones \$850,000.[^cite_note-CO-317] Bennett said the president made the settlement only so he could end the lawsuit for good and move on with his life.[^cite_note-clqc-318] During the deposition for the Jones lawsuit, which was held at the White House,[^cite_note-319] Clinton [denied having sexual relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton%E2%80%93Lewinsky_scandal) with Monica Lewinsky—a denial that became the basis for an impeachment charge of perjury.[^cite_note-320]

In 1998, [Kathleen Willey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathleen_Willey) alleged that Clinton had [groped](26052112.md) her in a hallway in 1993. An independent counsel determined Willey gave "false information" to the FBI, inconsistent with sworn testimony related to the Jones allegation.[^cite_note-wilo9r-321] On March 19, 1998, Julie Hiatt Steele, a friend of Willey, released an [affidavit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affidavit), accusing the former White House aide of asking her to lie to corroborate Ms. Willey's account of being sexually groped by Clinton in the Oval Office.[^cite_note-322] An attempt by Kenneth Starr to prosecute Steele for making false statements and obstructing justice ended in a mistrial and Starr declined to seek a retrial after Steele sought an investigation against the former independent counsel for prosecutorial misconduct.[^cite_note-323]

Also in 1998, [Juanita Broaddrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juanita_Broaddrick) alleged that Clinton had raped her in the spring of 1978, although she said she did not remember the exact date.[^cite_note-CapitalHillBlueWebArchive1-324] To support her charge, Broaddrick notes that she told multiple witnesses in 1978 she had been raped by Clinton, something these witnesses also state in interviews to the press.[^cite_note-vox.com-325] Broaddrick had earlier filed an affidavit denying any "unwelcome sexual advances" and later repeated the denial in a sworn deposition.[^cite_note-CapitalHillBlueWebArchive1-324] In a 1998 NBC interview wherein she detailed the alleged rape, Broaddrick said she had denied (under oath) being raped only to avoid testifying about the ordeal publicly.[^cite_note-CapitalHillBlueWebArchive1-324]

The Lewinsky scandal has had an enduring impact on Clinton's legacy, beyond his impeachment in 1998.[^cite_note-nytimes.com-326] In the wake of the #MeToo movement (which shed light on the widespread prevalence of [sexual assault](218407.md) and [harassment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_harassment), especially in the workplace), various commentators and Democratic political leaders, as well as Lewinsky herself, have revisited their view that the Lewinsky affair was consensual, and instead characterized it as an abuse of power or harassment, in light of the power differential between a president and a 22-year-old intern. In 2018, Clinton was asked in several interviews about whether he should have resigned, and he said he had made the right decision in not resigning.[^cite_note-\:0-327] During the [2018 congressional elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_U.S._election), *The New York Times* alleged that having no Democratic candidate for office asking Clinton to campaign with them was a change that attributed to the revised understanding of the Lewinsky scandal.[^cite_note-nytimes.com-326] However, former DNC interim chair [Donna Brazile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donna_Brazile) previously urged Clinton in November 2017 to campaign during the 2018 midterm elections, in spite of New York U.S. senator [Kirsten Gillibrand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirsten_Gillibrand)'s recent criticism of the Lewinsky scandal.[^cite_note-328]

### Relationship with Jeffrey Epstein

Main article: [Relationship of Bill Clinton and Jeffrey Epstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relationship_of_Bill_Clinton_and_Jeffrey_Epstein)



President Clinton hosting Epstein and Maxwell at the White House in 1993

Clinton also had [a relationship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relationship_of_Bill_Clinton_and_Jeffrey_Epstein) with the American financier and 2008-convicted child sex offender [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md) from 1993 to 2003 and made flights on Epstein's plane numerous times from 2002 to 2003 for charity trips, and sending him a message in his 2003 [birthday greeting album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Epstein's_birthday_book).[^cite_note-333] However, the details surrounding their relationship have been disputed, with Epstein's former romantic partner [Ghislaine Maxwell](32018562.md), who is acknowledged to have visited the White House with Epstein when Clinton was president, being described as the person who was the "glue" of Epstein and Clinton's relationship.[^cite_note-338]

[Virginia Giuffre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Giuffre) made controversial allegations that Clinton had visited [Epstein's private island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Saint_James).[^cite_note-\:2-339][^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] In a 2011 *[Daily Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Mail)* interview, she said that Ghislaine Maxwell "went to pick up Bill in a huge black helicopter that Jeffrey had bought her", and that "Bill had the Secret Service with him and I remember him talking about what a good job she did".[^cite_note-\:2-339] In a 2011 email Epstein sent, he wrote: "\[T]hese stories are complete an\[d] utter fantasy, I don't know and have never met Al gore, Clinton was never on the island".[^cite_note-342] [Alan Dershowitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alan_Dershowitz) argued Giuffre's story was improbable, as the secret service would not allow Maxwell, "then a novice helicopter pilot", to fly a former president.[^cite_note-343] A 2017 [Freedom of Information Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Information_Act_\(United_States\)) request found no evidence of secret service records to back claims that Clinton visited Epstein's island.[^cite_note-POLITICO-2017info-329] Giuffre later walked back claims that she witnessed Maxwell fly Clinton to the island.[^cite_note-ABC_News-344]

### Alleged affairs

Clinton has admitted to having extramarital affairs with singer [Gennifer Flowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gennifer_Flowers) and [Monica Lewinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_Lewinsky).[^cite_note-345] Actress [Elizabeth Gracen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Gracen),[^cite_note-346] Miss Arkansas winner [Sally Perdue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sally_Perdue),[^cite_note-347] and Dolly Kyle Browning[^cite_note-348] all claimed that they had affairs with Clinton during his time as Governor of Arkansas. Browning later sued Clinton, [Bruce Lindsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Lindsey), [Robert S. Bennett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_S._Bennett), and [Jane Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane_Mayer), alleging they engaged in a conspiracy to attempt to block her from publishing a book loosely based on her relationship with Clinton and tried to defame him. However, Browning's lawsuit was dismissed.[^cite_note-349]

## Post-presidency (2001–present)

Main article: [Post-presidency of Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-presidency_of_Bill_Clinton)

### Activities until 2008 campaign

In 2002, Clinton warned that pre-emptive military action against Iraq would have unwelcome consequences,[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] and later claimed to have opposed the [Iraq War](5043324.md) from the start (though some dispute this).[^cite_note-352] In 2005, Clinton criticized the [Bush administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush) for its handling of emissions control, while speaking at the [United Nations Climate Change conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Climate_Change_conference) in Montreal.[^cite_note-353]

The [William J. Clinton Presidential Center and Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_J._Clinton_Presidential_Center_and_Park) in [Little Rock, Arkansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Rock,_Arkansas), was dedicated in 2004.[^cite_note-354] Clinton released a best-selling autobiography, *[My Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Life_\(Clinton_autobiography\))*, in 2004.[^cite_note-355] Clinton's [official White House portrait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_White_House_portraits_of_Bill_and_Hillary_Clinton), commissioned by the [White House Historical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Historical_Association), was unveiled in June 2004. It was painted by [Simmie Knox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simmie_Knox).[^cite_note-NYT04-356] In 2007, he released *[Giving: How Each of Us Can Change the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giving:_How_Each_of_Us_Can_Change_the_World)*, which also became a [*New York Times* Best Seller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times_Best_Seller_list) and garnered positive reviews.[^cite_note-357]



Former presidents George H. W. Bush and Clinton in the White House Library, January 2005

In the aftermath of the [2004 Asian tsunami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Indian_Ocean_earthquake_and_tsunami), U.N. secretary-general [Kofi Annan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kofi_Annan) appointed Clinton to head a relief effort.[^cite_note-358] After [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina), Clinton joined with fellow former president George H. W. Bush to establish the Bush-Clinton Tsunami Fund in January 2005, and the Bush-Clinton Katrina Fund in October of that year.[^cite_note-359] As part of the tsunami effort, these two ex-presidents appeared in a [Super Bowl XXXIX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXXIX) pre-game show,[^cite_note-360] and traveled to the affected areas.[^cite_note-361] They also spoke together at the funeral of [Boris Yeltsin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_Yeltsin) in April 2007.[^cite_note-362]

Based on his philanthropic worldview,[^cite_note-363] Clinton created the [William J. Clinton Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_J._Clinton_Foundation) to address issues of global importance. This foundation includes the Clinton Foundation HIV and AIDS Initiative (CHAI), which strives to combat that disease, and has worked with the Australian government toward that end. The [Clinton Global Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_Foundation) (CGI), begun by the Clinton Foundation in 2005, attempts to address world problems such as global [public health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_health), poverty alleviation and religious and [ethnic conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_conflict).[^cite_note-364] In 2005, Clinton announced through his foundation an agreement with manufacturers to stop selling sugary drinks in schools.[^cite_note-365] Clinton's foundation joined with the [Large Cities Climate Leadership Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_Cities_Climate_Leadership_Group) in 2006 to improve cooperation among those cities, and he met with foreign leaders to promote this initiative.[^cite_note-366] The foundation has received donations from many governments all over the world, including Asia and the Middle East.[^cite_note-theage.com.au-367] In 2008, Foundation director [Inder Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inder_Singh_\(philanthropist\)) announced deals to reduce the price of anti-malaria drugs by 30 percent in developing nations.[^cite_note-368] Clinton also spoke in favor of [California Proposition 87](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Proposition_87_\(2006\)) on [alternative energy](533487.md), which was voted down.[^cite_note-369]

### 2008 presidential election



Clinton speaking at the [2008 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Democratic_National_Convention)

During the [2008 Democratic presidential primary campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_presidential_primaries,_2008), Clinton vigorously advocated on behalf of his wife, Hillary. Through speaking engagements and fundraisers, he was able to raise \$10 million toward her campaign.[^cite_note-370] Some worried that as an ex-president, he was too active on the trail, too negative to Clinton rival [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), and alienating his supporters at home and abroad.[^cite_note-CBS-Tarnish-371] Many were especially critical of him following his remarks in the South Carolina primary, which Obama won. Later in the 2008 primaries, there was some infighting between Bill and Hillary's staffs, especially in Pennsylvania.[^cite_note-autogenerated3-372] Considering Bill's remarks, many thought he could not rally Hillary supporters behind Obama after Obama won the primary.[^cite_note-373] Such remarks led to apprehension that the party would be split to the detriment of Obama's election. Fears were allayed August 27, 2008, when Clinton enthusiastically endorsed Obama at the [2008 Democratic National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Democratic_National_Convention), saying all his experience as president assures him that Obama is "ready to lead".[^cite_note-374] After Hillary Clinton's presidential campaign was over, Bill Clinton continued to raise funds to help pay off her campaign debt.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376]

### After the 2008 election

In 2009, Clinton travelled to [North Korea](21255.md) on behalf of two American journalists [imprisoned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_imprisonment_of_American_journalists_by_North_Korea) there. [Euna Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euna_Lee) and [Laura Ling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Ling) had been imprisoned for illegally entering the country from China.[^cite_note-BBCDPRK-377] [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter) had made a similar visit in 1994.[^cite_note-BBCDPRK-377] After Clinton met with North Korean leader [Kim Jong-il](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Jong-il), Kim issued a pardon.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379]

Since then, Clinton has been assigned many other diplomatic missions. He was named United Nations [Special Envoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Envoy_of_the_Secretary-General) to Haiti in 2009 following a series of hurricanes which caused \$1 billion in damages.[^cite_note-haiti-380] Clinton organized a conference with the Inter-American Development Bank, where a new industrial park was discussed in an effort to "build back better".[^cite_note-Sontag-381] In response to the [2010 Haiti earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Haiti_earthquake), U.S. president Barack Obama announced that Clinton and George W. Bush would coordinate efforts to raise funds for Haiti's recovery.[^cite_note-382] Funds began pouring into Haiti, which led to funding becoming available for Caracol Industrial Park in a part of the country unaffected by the earthquake. While Hillary Clinton was in South Korea, she and [Cheryl Mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheryl_Mills) worked to convince SAE-A, a large apparel subcontractor, to invest in Haiti despite the company's deep concerns about plans to raise the minimum wage. In the summer of 2010, the South Korean company signed a contract at the U.S. State Department, ensuring that the new industrial park would have a key tenant.[^cite_note-Sontag-381] In 2010, Clinton announced support of, and delivered the keynote address for, the inauguration of [NTR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTR_plc), Ireland's first environmental foundation.[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] At the 2012 Democratic National Convention, Clinton gave a widely praised speech nominating Barack Obama.[^cite_note-385]

### 2016 presidential election and after



Clinton campaigning at an election rally for his wife Hillary who was running for President of the United States, 2016

During the [2016 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_presidential_election), Clinton again encouraged voters to support Hillary, and made appearances speaking on the campaign trail.[^cite_note-386] In a series of tweets, then-president-elect [Donald Trump](4848272.md) criticized his ability to get people out to vote.[^cite_note-CBS-387] Clinton served as a member of the electoral college for the state of New York. He voted for the Democratic ticket consisting of his wife Hillary and her running-mate [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine).[^cite_note-388]

On September 7, 2017, Clinton partnered with former presidents Jimmy Carter, George H. W. Bush, George W. Bush, and Barack Obama to work with [One America Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_America_Appeal) to help the victims of [Hurricane Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Harvey) and [Hurricane Irma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Irma) in the [Gulf Coast](11969.md) and [Texas](29810.md) communities.[^cite_note-389]



Clinton with Vice President [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) and President [Joe Biden](145422.md) in February 2023

In [2020](48410011.md), Clinton again served as a member of the United States Electoral College from New York, casting his vote for the successful Democratic ticket of [Joe Biden](145422.md) and [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris).[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

Clinton was one of the first public figures to endorse Biden's re-election campaign in [2024](59568546.md), with him appearing in interviews and fundraisers with various politicians and national figures. He was also one of the most notable politicians to defend Biden after his critically maligned [first presidential debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Joe_Biden%E2%80%93Donald_Trump_presidential_debate) on June 27, with him stating that "bad debate nights happen" and continued to press support for him despite increasing demand from the public and Democratic party requesting for him to drop out.[^cite_note-392] After Biden [withdrew his candidacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Withdrawal_of_Joe_Biden_from_the_2024_United_States_presidential_election) and vice president Harris replaced him on the ticket, both Bill and Hillary Clinton endorsed her and praised Biden for his work in public service. Clinton later gave a critically acclaimed speech at the [2024 DNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Democratic_National_Convention), where he emphasized the Democratic Party's record on job creation and Harris' career achievements as a prosecutor, senator, and vice president.[^cite_note-393]

He later stumped for Harris at various battleground states, where he met with supporters in small towns and at campaign stops. At a stop in Michigan, Clinton caused a backlash by criticizing Arab and Muslim Americans hesitant to support Harris due to her pro-Israeli position, stating Israel had been "forced" to kill civilians during [its war in Gaza](74998519.md).[^cite_note-394] His comments led the [Institute for Middle East Understanding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Middle_East_Understanding) to state, "Bill Clinton's racist and ahistorical remarks were meant to justify the ethnic cleansing of Palestinians from their land. The Harris campaign is doing itself no favors attaching itself to that kind of hateful rhetoric".[^cite_note-395] He expanded on his comments in an interview with CNN shortly after, stating that he was trying to appeal to both sides of the issue and highlighted his work with Arafat and Rabin in the Oslo Accords, although his response still received sharp condemnation from Muslim, Arab, and Palestinian-Americans.[^cite_note-396]



Clinton in October 2024

After Harris lost the general election to Trump, Clinton's remarks were brought up by critics and pundits, who stated that they were the reason why Harris lost support among Arab-American voters and why she fared poorly in the Muslim-populated cities of [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan) and [Hamtramck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamtramck,_Michigan) when compared to previous Democratic candidates.[^cite_note-397] Critics also questioned his relevance to the modern Democratic Party, with pundits stating that his centrist policies and promotion of candidates who align with his views no longer work with a party that has tried to rebrand itself after losing support amongst progressive and populist supporters.[^cite_note-398] After the election, he and Hillary released a statement congratulating president-elect Trump and vice president-elect [JD Vance](53396477.md), while stating that Harris and her running-mate [Tim Walz](2216593.md) ran a "positive, forward-looking campaign to be proud of".[^cite_note-399]

Clinton's second volume of memoirs, *[Citizen: My Life After the White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizen_\(Bill_Clinton_book\))*, was published in November 2024.[^cite_note-400]

On November 20, 2025, Clinton returned to [Little Rock, Arkansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Rock,_Arkansas) to attend an event celebrating the 75th anniversary of the [Arkansas Governor's Mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arkansas_Governor's_Mansion), with Arkansas Governor [Sarah Huckabee Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Huckabee_Sanders) and other former governors, including U.S. ambassador [Mike Huckabee](301237.md) and [Asa Hutchinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asa_Hutchinson), in attendance.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] The next day, Bill and Hillary visited Rev. [Jesse Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_Jackson) at the [Northwestern Memorial Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Memorial_Hospital) in [Chicago](6886.md).[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404]

### Wealth

The Clintons incurred several million dollars in legal bills during his presidency, which were paid off four years after he left office.[^cite_note-405] Bill and Hillary Clinton have each earned millions of dollars from book publishing.[^cite_note-NYT-2008-04-05-406] In 2016, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* reported Bill and Hillary Clinton made about \$240 million in the 15 years from January 2001, to December 2015, (mostly from paid speeches, business consulting and book-writing).[^cite_note-407] Also in 2016, [CNN](62028.md) reported the Clintons combined to receive more than \$153 million in paid speeches from 2001 until spring 2015.[^cite_note-CNN-2006-02-06-408] In May 2015, *[The Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hill_\(newspaper\))* reported that Bill and Hillary Clinton have made more than \$25 million in speaking fees since the start of 2014, and that Hillary Clinton also made \$5 million or more from her book, *[Hard Choices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_Choices)*, during the same time period.[^cite_note-409] In July 2014, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported that at the end of 2012, the Clintons were worth between \$5 million and \$25.5 million, and that in 2012 (the last year they were required to disclose the information) the Clintons made between \$16 and \$17 million, mostly from speaking fees earned by the former president.[^cite_note-410] Clinton earned more than \$104 million from paid speeches between 2001 and 2012.[^cite_note-bloomberg.com-411] In June 2014, ABC News and *The Washington Post* reported that Bill Clinton has made more than \$100 million giving paid speeches since leaving public office, and in 2008, *The New York Times* reported that the Clintons' income tax returns[^cite_note-412] show they made \$109 million in the eight years from January 1, 2000, to December 31, 2007, including almost \$92 million from his speaking and book-writing.[^cite_note-416]

Clinton has given dozens of paid speeches each year since leaving office in 2001, mostly to corporations and philanthropic groups in North America and Europe; he often earned \$100,000 to \$300,000 per speech.[^cite_note-420] A Russian investment bank with ties to the Kremlin paid Clinton \$500,000 for a speech in [Moscow](19004.md).[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422] Hillary Clinton said she and Bill came out of the White House financially "broke" and in debt, especially due to large legal fees incurred during their years in the White House. "We had no money when we got there, and we struggled to, you know, piece together the resources for mortgages, for houses, for Chelsea's education". She added, "Bill has worked really hard ... we had to pay off all our debts ... he had to make double the money because of, obviously, taxes; and then pay off the debts, and get us houses, and take care of family members".[^cite_note-ABC-News-2014-06-09-414]

## Personal life

At the age of 10, he was baptized at Park Place [Baptist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baptist) Church in [Hot Springs, Arkansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hot_Springs,_Arkansas).[^cite_note-423] When he became president in 1993, he became a member of [Foundry United Methodist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundry_United_Methodist_Church) in [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) with his wife, a [Methodist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist).[^cite_note-424]

On October 11, 1975, in [Fayetteville, Arkansas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fayetteville,_Arkansas), he married [Hillary Rodham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton), whom he met while studying at [Yale University](34273.md). They had [Chelsea Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea_Clinton), their only child, on February 27, 1980.[^cite_note-425] He is the maternal grandfather to Chelsea's three children.[^cite_note-426]

### Health

In September 2004, Clinton underwent quadruple bypass surgery.[^cite_note-news.bbc.co.uk-427] In March 2005, he again underwent surgery, this time for a partially collapsed lung.[^cite_note-428] On February 11, 2010, he was rushed to [New York-Presbyterian/Columbia Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbia_University_Irving_Medical_Center) in Manhattan after complaining of chest pains, and he had two [coronary stents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronary_stent) implanted in his heart.[^cite_note-news.bbc.co.uk-427][^cite_note-429] After this procedure, Clinton adopted a plant-based whole foods ([vegan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegan)) diet, which had been recommended by doctors [Dean Ornish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dean_Ornish) and [Caldwell Esselstyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caldwell_Esselstyn).[^cite_note-cnn2011-430] He has since incorporated fish and lean animal flesh at the suggestion of [Mark Hyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Hyman_\(doctor\)), a proponent of the [pseudoscientific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pseudoscientific) ethos of [functional medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_medicine).[^cite_note-431] As a result, he is no longer strictly on a plant-based diet.[^cite_note-432] In 2010 Clinton lost 20 pounds for his daughter Chelsea's wedding.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

In October 2021, Clinton was treated for [sepsis](158400.md) at the [University of California, Irvine Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Irvine_Medical_Center).[^cite_note-439] In December 2022, Clinton tested positive for [COVID-19](63030231.md).[^cite_note-440] In December 2024, Clinton was hospitalized after developing a fever at the [MedStar Georgetown University Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MedStar_Georgetown_University_Hospital) in Washington D.C.[^cite_note-441][^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443] In August 2025, he was seen with a portable [defibrillator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defibrillation).[^cite_note-444]

## Accolades

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of awards and honors received by Bill Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_and_honors_received_by_Bill_Clinton).



Clinton receiving the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) from [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama), 2013

Several colleges and universities have awarded Clinton honorary degrees.[^cite_note-449] He is an honorary fellow of University College, Oxford, which he attended as a [Rhodes Scholar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhodes_Scholar), although he did not complete his studies there.[^cite_note-Hoffman-26][^cite_note-450] Schools have been named for Clinton,[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] and statues have been built of him.[^cite_note-WJCstatue-454][^cite_note-455] He was presented with the [Medal for Distinguished Public Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_for_Distinguished_Public_Service) in 2001.[^cite_note-456] The [Clinton Presidential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_Presidential_Center) was opened in Little Rock, Arkansas, in 2001.[^cite_note-NYT-457]

The Republic of Kosovo, in gratitude for his help during the [Kosovo War](16760.md), renamed a major street in the capital city of [Pristina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pristina) as [Bill Clinton Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Clinton_Boulevard) and added a Clinton statue.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] In 2011, Haitian president [Michel Martelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Martelly) awarded Clinton with the [National Order of Honour and Merit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Order_of_Honour_and_Merit) to the rank of Grand Cross "for his various initiatives in Haiti and especially his high contribution to the reconstruction of the country after the earthquake of January 12, 2010".[^cite_note-461]

Clinton was selected as *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))*'s "[Man of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Person_of_the_Year)" in 1992,[^cite_note-462] and again in 1998, along with [Ken Starr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Starr).[^cite_note-463] From a poll conducted of the American people in December 1999, Clinton was among eighteen included in [Gallup's List of Most Widely Admired People of the 20th Century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup's_List_of_Most_Widely_Admired_People_of_the_20th_Century).[^cite_note-464] In 2001, Clinton received the [NAACP President's Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NAACP_Image_Award_%E2%80%93_President's_Award).[^cite_note-465] He has also been honored with a J. William Fulbright Prize for International Understanding,[^cite_note-466] a [TED](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TED_\(conference\)) Prize,[^cite_note-467] and was named as an Honorary [GLAAD Media Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GLAAD_Media_Award) recipient for his work as an advocate for the LGBT community.[^cite_note-24-GLAAD-LA-468]

Clinton, along with [Mikhail Gorbachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev) and [Sophia Loren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Loren),[^cite_note-469] received the [2003 Grammy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/46th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) for [Best Spoken Word Album for Children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Spoken_Word_Album_for_Children) for *[Wolf Tracks and Peter and the Wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolf_Tracks_and_Peter_and_the_Wolf)*.[^cite_note-GRAMMY-470][^cite_note-471] The [audiobook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audiobook) edition of his autobiography, [*My Life*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Life_\(Clinton_autobiography\)), read by Clinton himself, won the [2005 Grammy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/47th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) for [Best Spoken Word Album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award_for_Best_Spoken_Word_Album),[^cite_note-GRAMMY-470] as well as the [Audie Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audie_Award) as the Audiobook of the Year.[^cite_note-472] Clinton has two more Grammy nominations for his audiobooks: *[Giving: How Each of Us Can Change the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giving:_How_Each_of_Us_Can_Change_the_World)* in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_Annual_Grammy_Awards) and *[Back to Work](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_to_Work_\(book\))* in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/55th_Annual_Grammy_Awards).[^cite_note-GRAMMY-470]

President Obama awarded Clinton the [Presidential Medal of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Medal_of_Freedom) on November 20, 2013.[^cite_note-Medal_of_Honor-473]
